are not of particular concern, the half-strip is denoted by
\sigma_{\eta}^{\varepsilon}
. The
base
of a half-strip
\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,\gamma}(x_{0},y_{0})
is the subset

\displaystyle\sigma^{\varepsilon}_{\eta,\gamma}(x_{0},y_{0})\cap\left\{(x,y)\,|\,\varepsilon y<\varepsilon y_{0}+\frac{\eta}{2}\right\}.

Consider a choice of (countably many) points
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
, of signs
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{+1,-1\}^{I}
, of positive numbers
\boldsymbol{\eta}\in\{\eta_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, and of continuous curves
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. Let
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x_{i},y_{i})
be the half-strip centered at
(x_{i},y_{i})
of sign
\varepsilon_{i}
and width
\eta_{i}>0
with bounding curve
\gamma_{i}
. Moreover, set

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:=\bigcup_{i}\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x_{i},y_{i}),

and denote the above choices of signs, widths and curves by the triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
.

Definition 5.28
.

Suppose
\mathsf{B}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
has the property that its intersection with any vertical line is either empty or path-connected, and consider a countable set of points
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
therein. A triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
as above is
admissible
for the subset
\mathsf{B}
relative to the points
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
if it satisfies the following conditions:

•

For all
i
, the base of the half-strip
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x_{i},y_{i})
is contained in
\operatorname{Int}(\mathsf{B})
.

•

If
(x_{i},y_{i})\in\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}(x_{j},y_{j})
for
i\neq j
, then
x_{i}=x_{j}
.

•

Whenever the half-strips
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x_{i},y_{i})
and
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}(x_{j},y_{j})
share the same center line,
\eta_{i}=\eta_{j}
.

•

The intersection of any two distinct half-strips is either empty or equal to one of the half-strips.

In this case, the corresponding half-strips are called
admissible
for
\mathsf{B}
relative to the points
\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
.

Examples of admissible half-strips are sketched in Fig.
5.4
(a) and (b).

Figure 5.4:
The symbol
\star
indicates the points at which the half-strips are centered. Figures (a) and (b) show admissible half-strips with the same center line. Figure (c) shows half-strips that are not admissible.

Definition 5.29
.

Let
(M,\omega,\Phi)
be a faithful semitoric system whose set of focus-focus values is indexed by
I
as in equation (
5.3
). A triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
as in Definition
5.28
and their corresponding half-strips are
admissible
for
(M,\omega,\Phi)
if they are admissible for
B
relative to the set of focus-focus values
B_{\mathrm{ff}}=\{c_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.

Before establishing the existence of admissible half-strips for any faithful semitoric system (Proposition
5.31
), we derive the following necessary condition. Recall that the focus-focus values of a faithful semitoric system are ordered as in Section
5.1
.

Proposition 5.30
.

If
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is an admissible triple for
(M,\omega,\Phi)
, then
S_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=B\smallsetminus l^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is contractible.

Proof.

Fix an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
. By Lemma
5.8
and Corollary
5.9
, it suffices to check that, if
i>j
and there are focus-focus points
c_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i})
,
c_{j}=(x_{j},y_{j})
with
x_{i}=x_{j}
, then
\varepsilon_{i}\geq\varepsilon_{j}
. Suppose not, then the half-strips
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x_{i},y_{i})
,
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}(x_{j},y_{j})
intersect, but neither is contained in the other (see Fig.
5.4
(c)), thus contradicting admissibility of the given triple.
∎

Recall that, by Corollary
5.9
,
S_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is path-connected if and only if it is contractible. The next result establishes the converse to Proposition
5.30
.

Proposition 5.31
.

Given a faithful semitoric system
(M,\omega,\Phi)
with at least one focus-focus value and any choice
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{+1,-1\}^{I}
making
S_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
path-connected, there exist a choice of positive numbers
\boldsymbol{\eta}=\{\eta_{i}\}_{i\in I}
and of continuous curves
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that the triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is admissible for
(M,\omega,\Phi)
. Moreover, the widths
\boldsymbol{\eta}
can be chosen so that if a half-strip
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i}}
contains a corner of
B
, then it contains precisely one, and that corner lies on the center line of the half-strip.

Proof.

Fix a choice of
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{+1,-1\}^{I}
as above and let
\{c_{i}=(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
denote the set of focus-focus values of
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
. By Proposition
4.18
, the set of first coordinates of focus-focus values is a subset of the set of critical values of
J
, which, by property
(F5)
, does not contain any limit point in
J(M)
. Moreover, focus-focus values are discrete in
B
and, by property
(F6)
, there are finitely many of them on a given vertical line. The above facts imply that there exists a choice of positive numbers
\boldsymbol{\eta}=\{\eta_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that

•

if
x_{i}=x_{j}
,
\eta_{i}=\eta_{j}
,

•

if, for
i\neq j
,
x_{j}\in\big[x_{i}-\frac{\eta_{i}}{2},x_{i}+\frac{\eta_{i}}{2}\big]
, then
x_{i}=x_{j}
, and

•

for all
i
,
\big[x_{i}-\frac{\eta_{i}}{2},x_{i}+\frac{\eta_{i}}{2}\big]\times\big]\varepsilon_{i}y_{i}-\frac{\eta_{i}}{2},\varepsilon_{i}y_{i}+\frac{\eta_{i}}{2}\big[
is contained in
\operatorname{Int}(B)
.

For each
i\in I
, define
\gamma_{i}\colon\big[x_{i}-\frac{\eta_{i}}{2},x_{i}+\frac{\eta_{i}}{2}\big]\to\mathbb{R}
to be
\gamma_{i}(x):=y_{i}-\varepsilon_{i}\frac{\eta}{2}
, and set
\boldsymbol{\gamma}=\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
. It can be checked that the triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is admissible for
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
. Moreover, if the
\eta_{i}
’s are chosen to be sufficiently small then any half-strip that contains a corner contains precisely one, which lies on the center line of the strip.
∎

Henceforth, any admissible triple for a faithful semitoric system is assumed to satisfy the final property in Proposition
5.31
unless otherwise stated. Corollary
5.10
and Proposition
5.31
readily yield the following result, stated below without proof.

Corollary 5.32
.

Every faithful semitoric system has an admissible triple.

Fix an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
for
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
. The next results can be interpreted as showing that the complement of the corresponding half-strips in
B
behaves like
S_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
.

Lemma 5.33
.

If
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is admissible for a faithful semitoric system
(M,\omega,\Phi)
, then
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is open in
B
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Corollary
5.6
, it suffices to show that
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is closed in
B
. Let
\{(x_{n},y_{n})\}\subset\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
be a sequence that converges to
(x_{0},y_{0})\in B
and consider the sequence
\{x_{n}\}=\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\{(x_{n},y_{n})\})
which converges to
x_{0}\in J(M)
. Since
J(M)
is locally compact, there exists a compact neighborhood
K\subset J(M)
of
x_{0}
. Since
x_{n}\to x_{0}
, it follows that all but finitely many of the
x_{n}
are contained in
K
. Since
K
is compact and the critical values of
J
are discrete in
J(M)
by property
(F5)
,
K
contains at most finitely many critical values of
J
. Therefore, by property
(F6)
and Proposition
4.18
, there are at most finitely many focus-focus values contained in
\operatorname{pr}^{-1}_{1}(K)\cap B
. Hence, all but finitely many of the
(x_{n},y_{n})
are contained in the union of finitely many admissible half-strips, each of which is a closed subset of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and, hence, of
B
. Thus
(x_{0},y_{0})
belongs to this union of finitely many admissible half-strips and so to
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
.
∎

Corollary 5.34
.

If
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is admissible for a faithful semitoric system
(M,\omega,\Phi)
, then
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is contractible.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.33
the subset
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
is open in
B
. If the intersection of
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
with every vertical line were either empty or connected, then Proposition
4.7
would imply that the subsystem relative to
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
would be faithful semitoric, after which Corollary
4.5
would ensure that
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, the moment map image of that faithful semitoric subsystem, would be contractible. Thus it suffices to show that the intersection of
B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
with every vertical line is either empty or connected. Fix
x_{0}\in\operatorname{pr}_{1}(B)
; if
x_{0}\notin\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
,

\displaystyle\big(B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\big)\cap\{(x,y)\,|\,x=x_{0}\}=B\cap\{(x,y)\,|\,x=x_{0}\},

and the result follows from the fact that
(M,\omega,\Phi)
is faithful semitoric. Suppose that
x_{0}\in\operatorname{pr}_{1}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
and call a half-strip
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{k}}_{\eta_{k},\gamma_{k}}(x_{k},y_{k})

maximal
if it is not a proper subset of
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}(x_{j},y_{j})
for any
j\neq k
. Because the triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is admissible for
(M,\omega,\Phi)
, there are at most two maximal half-strips
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{i}}_{\eta_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x_{i},y_{i})
,
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}(x_{j},y_{j})
with the property that for
s=i,j
,
x_{0}\in\big[x_{s}-\frac{\eta_{s}}{2},x_{s}+\frac{\eta_{s}}{2}\big]
. These half-strips are, by definition, disjoint and their bases are contained in
\operatorname{Int}(B)
. That property is sufficient to ensure that
\big(B\smallsetminus\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\big)\cap\{(x,y)\,|\,x=x_{0}\}
is connected, as desired.
∎

Finally, we note that admissible triples behave well under isomorphisms of strict complexity one systems and taking saturated subsystems.

Corollary 5.35
.

Let
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
be an admissible triple for the faithful semitoric system
(M,\omega,\Phi)
. Then any faithful semitoric system isomorphic to
(M,\omega,\Phi)
as a strict complexity one system inherits an admissible triple, as does any subsystem whose image contains every half-strip of
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
that it intersects. Moreover, for any cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
associated to
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
,
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
induces an admissible triple for
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(B)
relative to the image of the focus-focus values
\{f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(c_{i})\}_{i\in I}
.

Proof.

Let
(M^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})
be a faithful semitoric system isomorphic to
(M,\omega,\Phi)
via the isomorphism
(\Psi,\psi)
as strict complexity one systems (see Definition
4.12
). The choices
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon^{\prime}}=\operatorname{sgn}(\det D\psi)\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
,
\boldsymbol{\eta^{\prime}}=\boldsymbol{\eta}
and
\boldsymbol{\gamma^{\prime}}=\psi\circ\boldsymbol{\gamma}:=\{\psi\circ\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
define an admissible triple for
(M^{\prime},\omega^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime})
because of the special form of
\psi
(see Definition
4.1
) and the connectedness of
B
, ensuring that
\operatorname{sgn}(\det D\psi)
is constant. Any faithful semitoric system whose image contains the half-strips of
\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
that it intersects inherits an admissible triple simply by restriction. Finally, given a cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, the signs
\boldsymbol{\tilde{\varepsilon}}=\operatorname{sgn}(f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
, widths
\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}=\boldsymbol{\eta}
and continuous curves
\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}}=f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\boldsymbol{\gamma}:=\{f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in I}
define an admissible triple for
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(B)
relative to
\{f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(c_{i})\}_{i\in i}
.
∎

5.3.2
Smoothing

With admissible half-strips at hand, we can state and prove the main result of this section.

Theorem 5.36
.

Let
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
be a faithful semitoric system and let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
be a choice of signs such that
B\smallsetminus l^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is connected.
(
The existence of one such
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
is guaranteed by Corollary
5.10
.)
Given any cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\colon B\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
, there exists a smooth embedding
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\colon B\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
of the form

\displaystyle F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=\big(F^{(1)}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},F^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)(x,y)=\big(x,F^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x,y)\big)

agreeing with
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
on the complement of an arbitrarily small neighborhood of
l^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
be as in the statement and fix an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
for
(M,\omega,\Phi)
. The map
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is smooth on the complement of the cuts. As in the proof of Corollary
5.34
, say that a half-strip is maximal if it is not a proper subset of any other half-strip. It is sufficient to modify
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
in the interior of maximal admissible half-strips, and since maximal half-strips are pairwise disjoint, it suffices to construct the modified map in the interior of each one separately.

Consider a maximal admissible half-strip, say
\sigma^{\varepsilon_{j}}_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}(x_{j},y_{j})
. Without loss of generality assume that
\varepsilon_{j}=+1
so as to drop the notational dependence of the half-strip on
\varepsilon_{j}
. Moreover, fix an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime})
for
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
, where, if
i\neq j
,
\eta^{\prime}_{i}=\eta_{i}
and
\gamma^{\prime}_{i}=\gamma_{i}
, and if
i=j
and
\eta^{\prime}_{j}<\eta_{j}
and
\gamma_{j}^{\prime}(x)>\gamma_{j}(x)
whenever both make sense.

There are two cases to consider, namely if
\eta^{\prime}_{j}
can be chosen so that
\partial B\cap\sigma_{\eta^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}}=\varnothing
or not. Suppose that the former holds; then the set
W_{j}=B\cap\operatorname{Int}(\sigma_{\eta^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}})
is open in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The situation is sketched in Fig.
5.5
(a). Let
\Gamma_{j}
be an embedded curve in
W_{j}
of the form
\Gamma_{j}(x)=(x,h_{j}(x))
, where
h_{j}
is a smooth function, that is disjoint from the cut
l^{\varepsilon_{j}}
and is such that
W_{j}\smallsetminus\Gamma_{j}
has two components. Let
K_{j}
,
L_{j}
be the closures in
W_{j}
of the two components of
W_{j}\smallsetminus\Gamma_{j}
, so
K_{j}\cap L_{j}=\Gamma_{j}
, and assume without loss of generality that the cut
l^{\varepsilon_{j}}
lies in
K_{j}
.

Recall that the cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
preserves orientation and is of the special form

\displaystyle f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x,y)=\big(f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(1)}(x,y),f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(2)}(x,y)\big)=\big(x,f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(2)}(x,y)\big).

Therefore,
f\circ\Gamma_{j}(x)=\big(x,f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(2)}(x,h_{j}(x))\big)
and, since
\varepsilon_{j}=+1
, if
(x,y)\in K_{j}
then
y\geq h_{j}(x)
and
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(2)}(x,y)\geq f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{(2)}(x,h_{j}(x))
, as
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is orientation-preserving. Define
g_{j}\colon K_{j}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
by

\displaystyle g_{j}(x,y):=\big(x,y+f^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x,h_{j}(x))-h_{j}(x)\big),

which is an orientation-preserving diffeomorphism of
K_{j}
onto its image that satisfies

\displaystyle g_{j}(x,h_{j}(x))=f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x,h_{j}(x)).

(5.10)

Now consider the map

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{j}}\colon\ {\rm Int}(\sigma_{\eta^{\prime}_{j},\gamma^{\prime}_{j}})\cap B\to\mathbb{R}^{2},\qquad(x,y)\mapsto\begin{cases}f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x,y)&\mbox{if }(x,y)\in L_{j}\\
g_{j}(x,y)&\mbox{if }(x,y)\in K_{j},\end{cases}

which is a homeomorphism onto its image by equation (
5.10
). Furthermore, because
F^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{j}}
is a diffeomorphism on the complement of
\Gamma_{j}
, which is a closed submanifold of
W_{j}
,
F^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{j}}
can be isotoped to be a diffeomorphism onto the image
F^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{j}}(W_{j})
via an isotopy that is supported in an arbitrarily small neighborhood of
\Gamma_{j}
and is the identity in
L_{j}
(cf. Hirsch
[
19
, Chapter 8]
). By construction,
F^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{j}}
extends to all of
\sigma_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}
as a diffeomorphism, say
F_{\varepsilon_{j}}
, on
\sigma_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}
that agrees with
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
on
\sigma_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}}\smallsetminus K_{j}
. The map
F_{\varepsilon_{j}}
is the desired smoothing.

It remains to consider the case in which an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{\prime},\boldsymbol{\gamma}^{\prime})
as above does not exist, i.e., for any choices of
\eta_{j}^{\prime}
and
\gamma_{j}^{\prime}
as above, the corresponding half-strip also intersects
\partial B
(see Fig.
5.5
(b)). In this case, modify the argument as follows. Let
\Gamma_{j}\subset B\cap{\rm Int}(\sigma_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}})
be chosen as above, and so that all boundary points of
\Gamma_{j}
also lie in path-connected components of
\partial B_{0}\cap{\rm Int}(\sigma_{\eta_{j},\gamma_{j}})
. Because
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is, by definition, smooth at a boundary point
p
of
\Gamma_{j}
, the map
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
and the smooth curve
\Gamma_{j}
can be extended to a neighborhood of
p\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Make such an extension near the one or two boundary points of
\Gamma
, and let
W_{j}
be an open tubular neighborhood of the extended curve
\Gamma_{j}
. Let
K_{j}
and
L_{j}
be defined as in the first case (enlarged as per the extension just described), with the map
F^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{j}}
defined as above. But to apply the smoothing argument, restrict attention to
K_{j}\cap W_{j}
and
L_{j}\cap W_{j}
so that
\Gamma_{j}
is a closed submanifold of an open manifold, in this case the tubular neighborhood
W_{j}
.
∎

Figure 5.5:
In the above figures,
K_{j}
is the darkest region, while
L_{j}
is the region whose shading is ‘medium’. Consider the curve
\Gamma_{j}
as ‘seam’ and glue the diffeomorphisms smoothly along
\Gamma_{j}
. (a) sketches the case
\partial B_{0}\cap\sigma_{j}^{\prime}=\varnothing
and (b) sketches
\partial B_{0}\cap\sigma_{j}^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
.

Definition 5.37
.

Given an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
, a map
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
as constructed in Theorem
5.36
is referred to as an
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic embedding
. Moreover, if the dependence on
\boldsymbol{\eta}
is to be remembered, an
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic embedding is denoted by
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}
.

Theorem
5.36
motivates introducing the following notion.

Definition 5.38
.

A faithful semitoric system is
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic
if it admits an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
and a cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
whose restriction to the complement of the union of the corresponding admissible half-strips is the identity. If the choice of
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is to be remembered, the system is said to be
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic
with respect to
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
.

The first application of Theorem
5.36
is the following result.

Theorem 5.39
.

Any faithful semitoric system
(M,\omega,\Phi)
is isomorphic, as a semitoric system, to an
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic one.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.32
,
(M,\omega,\Phi)
has an admissible triple. Fix one such triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
for
(M,\omega,\Phi)
and let
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\colon B\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
be the cartographic homeomorphism associated to
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
. Let
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\colon B\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
be the associated smooth
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic embedding constructed in the proof of Theorem
5.36
. The form of
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
implies that, by construction,
(M,\omega,F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\Phi)
is a faithful semitoric system isomorphic to
(M,\omega,\Phi)
. Moreover, since
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is orientation-preserving,
(M,\omega,F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\Phi)
inherits an admissible triple
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\boldsymbol{\gamma})
by Corollary
5.35
. The map
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{-1}\colon F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(B)\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
is a cartographic homeomorphism for
(M,\omega,F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\Phi)
, which, by definition of
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, is the identity on the complement of the admissible half-strips for
(M,\omega,F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\Phi)
corresponding to
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\boldsymbol{\gamma})
. Therefore,
(M,\omega,F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\circ\Phi)
is
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic as required.
∎

To conclude this section, we show that the image of a cartographic homeomorphism can be seen as a ‘limit’ of the moment map images of
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic systems. To make the above precise, let
(M,\omega,\Phi)
be a faithful semitoric system, fix a choice of signs
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
for which
S_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is path-connected (which exists by Corollary
5.10
), and fix a cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
associated to
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
. Consider the set consisting of quadruples
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})
, where
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma})
is an admissible triple for
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
and
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}
is an
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic embedding constructed starting from the cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
. On this set, we define a partial order
\succeq
by setting

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})\succeq(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}}),

if and only if
\eta_{j}\geq{\widetilde{\eta}}_{j}
for all
j
, and
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\supseteq F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}}(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})
. Note that
\succeq
is reflexive and transitive. Moreover, for any two elements
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})
and
\big(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}}\big)
, there exists an element
\big(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\eta}}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\gamma}}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\eta}}}}\big)
such that
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})\succeq\big(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\eta}}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\gamma}}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\eta}}}}\big)
and
\big(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}}\big)\succeq\big(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\eta}}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\gamma}}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\tilde{\eta}}}}\big)
,
as the construction of
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}
and
F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}}
shows. Thus
\succeq
turns the set of quadruples
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})
into a directed set.

Proposition 5.40
.

A cartographic moment map image of a faithful semitoric system associated to a choice of signs whose corresponding cuts do not disconnect the moment map image is the direct limit of
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-cartographic moment map images.

Proof.

As above, fix a faithful semitoric system
(M,\omega,\Phi=(J,H))
, a choice of signs
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
making
S_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
path-connected, and a cartographic homeomorphism
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
associated to
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
. The set of quadruples
(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})
is a directed set with the above partial order
\succeq
. The condition

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta},\boldsymbol{\gamma},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}})\succeq\big(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}},F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}}\big)

implies that

\displaystyle F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}\big(B\smallsetminus{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)\subseteq F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\tilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}}}\big(B\smallsetminus{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\tilde{\boldsymbol{\eta}},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)

(5.11)

such that
f_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(B\smallsetminus l^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})
coincides with the direct limit in the category of topological spaces given by

\displaystyle\lim_{\longrightarrow}F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}\big(B\smallsetminus{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)=\left.\left(\bigsqcup_{\boldsymbol{\eta}}F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}\big(B\smallsetminus{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)\right)\right/\sim,

where
z\in F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\eta}}\big(B\smallsetminus{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\eta},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)\sim{\widetilde{z}}\in F_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon},\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}}}\big(B\smallsetminus{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\tilde{\eta}},{\boldsymbol{\tilde{\gamma}}}}^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\big)
if
z
and
{\widetilde{z}}
get mapped under the corresponding inclusions in (
5.11
) to the same point.
∎
